# Peanut Oil

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="peanut-oil-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="peanut-oil-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="peanut-oil-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Peanut Oil in the HMI library has 55 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, tin, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, As and Ba from 6 sources.<sup For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the peanut Oil came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as peanut Oil

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers peanut Oil in 2 forms kept separate in every comparison: peanut Oil, oil (53 results) and peanut Oil, as sold (2 results).<sup <div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-peanut-oil" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-peanut-oil-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of peanut oil, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.

<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Peanut Oil FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-peanut-oil" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-peanut-oil" data-question="What heavy metals are found in peanut Oil?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in peanut Oil?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, tin, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, As and Ba in peanut Oil, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/ashraf2012-heavy-metals-vegetable-oils-saudi-arabia|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ashraf MW · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ashraf2012-heavy-metals-vegetable-oils-saudi-arabia">Levels of Selected Heavy Metals in Varieties of Vegetable Oils Consumed in Kingdom of Taiwan</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">JSFA Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/lin2024-edible-oils-taiwan-12-metals">Read source <details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-peanut-oil-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-peanut-oil-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in peanut Oil is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in peanut Oil is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 1 result from 1 source, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported inorganic arsenic in peanut Oil, oil from 20 results in 1 reporting region, with subgroup medians below 40 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
